<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01FHmYd7XAHek67ya2s5vARG`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Why the router fails: task-type-level signal.}
5	The router diagnostic (\S\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}) reveals a structural mismatch: death avoidance is a task-type-level binary property (10/11 types carry death risk), but step-level features correlate weakly because death risk depends on multi-step trajectories. The 4B router improves at episode level ($59.0\%$) without step-level gains ($56$--$59\%$), confirming task-type classification; cross-task-type transfer yields chance accuracy (49.2\%; Appendix~\ref{app:ablations}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Non-triviality of death dominance.}
8	While death dominance follows from the penalty structure (\S\ref{sec:daf}), three specific consequences were not predictable a priori: (i)~$10{\times}$ scaling to 72B fails to eliminate it (DAF${}= 92.4\%$); (ii)~Reflexion self-correction yields DAF${}>100\%$, meaning death avoidance \emph{more} than accounts for the full gap; (iii)~a 0.5B model shows \emph{reduced} death rate ($13.3\%$; $n{=}15$) because it is too weak to reach dangerous states, revealing an inverted-U capability--vulnerability relationship. All four intervention axes leave ${\geq}12$~pp residual gaps to deterministic safety (Appendix~\ref{sec:threshold-routing}); cross-model stability ($36$--$43\%$ internal DR across three families) confirms death vulnerability is a task-environment property. More aggressive interventions remain untested, so we characterize a ceiling, not a universal bound.
9	
10	\paragraph{Anatomy of catastrophic failure.}
11	All 105 internal-strategy deaths trace to a single grounding failure: misuse of the answer-submission action (\texttt{focus on}) as a general interaction command. The internal strategy enters catastrophic states more frequently (105 vs.\ 40 deaths), not through different within-episode behavior; grounding interventions reduce but do not eliminate death (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}--\ref{app:grounding-ablation}).
12	
13	\paragraph{Practical implications.}
14	Death dominance diagnosis is actionable: a ${\sim}$50-episode pilot under pure strategies suffices to compute $\Delta\text{DR}$ and $f_{\text{death}}$, classifying the environment before committing to router development (Appendix~\ref{app:decision-framework}). In ScienceWorld, where $f_{\text{death}} = 78.9\%$ and all four validated conditions confirm the prediction, the optimal strategy is fixed deterministic routing---no learned router required. More broadly, our decomposition provides a build-vs-buy diagnostic: environments with $f_{\text{death}} > 75\%$ gain nothing from routing; those with $f_{\text{death}} < 50\%$ present genuine routing opportunities warranting investment in learned routers.
15	
16	
17	
```

    "oldString": "In ScienceWorld, a single mechanism---death avoidance---accounts for 78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels, stable across three model families (DAF${=}88$--$90\\%$); our episode-level counterfactual framework diagnoses this before any router is built, challenging the routing literature's implicit assumption of diverse signal sources. Actually, I could be more aggressive and compress the Conclusion down to a single sentence, removing the cross-environment stability mention since that's already covered in the contributions and discussion sections. That would save significant space while keeping the core finding about the death-avoidance mechanism accounting for nearly 79% of routing labels.

_source line 1295 · 2026-05-21T11:49:10.716Z_

[Reviewer 回复]:
## 审查报告 — Discussion 写作改进 + 全文叙事一致性

**结论**: WARNING（1 个 warning + 多个 info，整体可改进但不阻塞）

---

### 1. 内容质量

**[severity: warning] "Practical implications" 段阈值缺 caveat**

Discussion L14 直接使用 f_death > 75% 和 < 50% 作为决策边界：
> "When f_death > 75% ... fixed deterministic routing is optimal; below 50%, genuine routing opportunities warrant learned routers."

但 Appendix R §Threshold calibration（main.tex L747）明确说：
> "The thresholds above (15pp, 75%, 50%) are illustrative, derived from ScienceWorld's specific reward structure... Practitioners should calibrate these boundaries for their specific environment."

**问题**：主文 Discussion 把 illustrative 阈值当成了 definitive guidance。审稿人若同时读到两处，会认为这是内部矛盾——主文声称 "75% 则 optimal"，Appendix 说 "75% 只是 illustrative"。

**建议修改**：在 Discussion 段加一个轻量 caveat，不需长篇：
```
When $f_{\text{death}} > 75\%$ (as in ScienceWorld, where all four
conditions validate; thresholds are environment-specific,
see Appendix~\ref{app:decision-framework}), fixed deterministic
routing is optimal; ...
```
或更简洁地把 "optimal" 改为 "competitive with or exceeds learned routing"——"optimal" 是 strong claim，论文只证明了 fixed-det 在 ScienceWorld 上 beats 训练的 router，没有证明它是所有可能策略中最优的。

**[severity: info] "all four conditions validate" 中 MATH 分类的宽松性**

Table 20（Appendix R）列出 MATH 的 prediction 为 "router viable"，actual 为 "no signal"。框架预测 "routing may be justified"（因为 f_death < 50%），但实际是 "根本没有 routing signal"。这是一个 ambiguous hit——框架正确说 "death 不是问题"，但给了过度乐观的 follow-up 建议（"may justify router"）。作为 4/4 retrospective validation 的一部分，审稿人可能质疑这算不算 "correctly classified"。

不需要修改，但如果审稿人追问，需要准备好 rebuttal 点："框架诊断信号来源（death vs non-death），不预测信号是否存在。MATH 的 f_death = 0% 正确排除了 death dominance。"

---

### 2. 叙事一致性

**[severity: info] 无重大 narrative mismatch。**

逐层检查：
| 章节 | 当前 framing | 与 Discussion "Practical implications" 兼容性 |
|------|-------------|----------------------------------------------|
| Abstract | "enabling informed build-vs-buy decisions" | ✓ 已有 prescriptive 元素 |
| Introduction | "predicts routing value from a ~50-episode pilot (validated 4/4)" | ✓ 对齐 |
| Experiments | 纯 diagnostic/analytical | ✓ 不冲突，Discussion 是自然的"so what" |
| Related Work | "we ask a prior question: what signal drives the routing decision?" | ✓ diagnostic framing 一致 |
| Conclusion | "~50-episode pilot informs build-vs-buy decisions" | ✓ 对齐 |

Abstract 和 Introduction 已经种下了 prescriptive 的种子（"build-vs-buy decisions"、"predicts routing value"），Discussion 新段是自然延伸，不是 narrative 转向。

**唯一的轻微张力**：Abstract 措辞谨慎（"enabling informed decisions"），Discussion 措辞更强（"is optimal"）。建议两边语气统一——要么都用 "fixed deterministic routing matches or exceeds learned routers" 的审慎措辞，要么都用"optimal"。鉴于 Appendix R 的 illustrative caveat，建议统一偏审慎。

---

### 3. 压缩后信息损失评估

**[severity: info] "Anatomy of catastrophic failure" 压缩：可接受**

被移除的具体数字：~5% `focus on` rate, 48.0% → 29.0% grounding intervention 效果。
- 保留了：105 deaths, grounding failure root cause, 105 vs 40 对比, appendix 引用
- 被移除的 "agents parse task descriptions correctly but cannot map understanding to valid primitives" 是对 grounding failure 的有用解释，但 "single grounding failure" 已传达核心意思
- **结论**：关键 claim 均保留，细节正确下沉到 appendix。无重要信息损失。

**[severity: warning] Conclusion 压缩丢失了有意义的词**

原文：
> "Cross-environment probes confirm the framework's generality, and a ~50-episode **diagnostic** pilot **suffices** to inform build-vs-buy decisions **in new environments**."

压缩后：
> "Cross-environment probes confirm generality; a ~50-episode pilot informs build-vs-buy decisions."

三个被删词的语义价值：
- **"diagnostic"**：标记 pilot 的性质（诊断而非训练），与论文核心 contribution 呼应
- **"suffices"**：传达效率（"只需要 50 集就够了"），这是 actionable takeaway 的关键
- **"in new environments"**：传达 generality，与前半句 "confirm generality" 呼应

**建议**：恢复至少 "suffices" 和 "in new environments"：
> "Cross-environment probes confirm generality; a ~50-episode diagnostic pilot suffices to inform build-vs-buy decisions in new environments."

Conclusion 只有 2 句，每个词都 load-bearing。省 6 个词换来的空间微乎其微，但信息损失明显。

---

### 4. Limitations "Diagnostic scope" 段

**[severity: info] 措辞准确，定位恰当。**

"diagnostic rather than prescriptive" 主动承认了最可能的 reviewer 攻击点，"combining DAF analysis with online policy learning" 给出了具体的 future work 方向。不需要修改。

---

### 5. 页面验证

**无法确认**——当前 PDF（main.pdf 时间戳 10:20）早于 tex 修改。需要重新编译后检查第 8 页是否仍然容纳 Conclusion。基于改动量估计（catastrophic 段净减 ~5 行，practical implications 段净增 ~5 行），页面应该持平或略紧。建议编译后目视检查。

---

### 总结 & 优先级

| 优先级 | 问题 | 建议 |
|--------|------|------|
| P0 | Discussion "optimal" + 阈值无 caveat | 加 "thresholds are environment-specific" caveat，"optimal" → "competitive with or exceeds" |
| P1 | Conclusion 恢复 "suffices" + "in new environments" | 一行改动，信息量显著提升 |
| P2 | 编译后检查第 8 页完整性 | 目视确认 Conclusion 未溢出 |

    "oldString": "When $f_{\\text{death}} > 75\\%$ (as in ScienceWorld, where all four conditions validate), fixed deterministic routing is optimal; below $50\\%$, genuine routing opportunities warrant learned routers.",
    "newString": "When $f_{\\text{death}} > 75\\%$ (as in ScienceWorld, where all four conditions validate; thresholds are environment-specific, Appendix~\\ref{app:decision-framework}), fixed deterministic routing matches or exceeds learned alternatives; below $50\\%$, genuine opportunities warrant router development.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Why the router fails: task-type-level signal.}\nThe router diagnostic (\\S\\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}) reveals a structural mismatch: death avoidance is a task-type-level binary property (10/11 types carry death risk), but step-level features correlate weakly because death risk depends on multi-step trajectories. The 4B router improves at episode level ($59.0\\%$) without step-level gains ($56$--$59\\%$), confirming task-type classification; cross-task-type transfer yields chance accuracy (49.2\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:ablations}).\n\n\\paragraph{Non-triviality of death dominance.}\nWhile death dominance follows from the penalty structure (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}), three specific consequences were not predictable a priori: (i)~$10{\\times}$ scaling to 72B fails to eliminate it (DAF${}= 92.4\\%$); (ii)~Reflexion self-correction yields DAF${}>100\\%$, meaning death avoidance \\emph{more} than accounts for the full gap; (iii)~a 0.5B model shows \\emph{reduced} death rate ($13.3\\%$; $n{=}15$) because it is too weak to reach dangerous states, revealing an inverted-U capability--vulnerability relationship. All four intervention axes leave ${\\geq}12$~pp residual gaps to deterministic safety (Appendix~\\ref{sec:threshold-routing}); cross-model stability ($36$--$43\\%$ internal DR across three families) confirms death vulnerability is a task-environment property. More aggressive interventions remain untested, so we characterize a ceiling, not a universal bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Anatomy of catastrophic failure.}\nAll 105 internal-strategy deaths trace to a single grounding failure: misuse of the answer-submission action (\\texttt{focus on}) as a general interaction command. The internal strategy enters catastrophic states more frequently (105 vs.\\ 40 deaths), not through different within-episode behavior; grounding interventions reduce but do not eliminate death (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}--\\ref{app:grounding-ablation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Practical implications.}\nDeath dominance diagnosis is actionable: a ${\\sim}$50-episode pilot suffices to compute $f_{\\text{death}}$ and classify the environment before committing to router development (Appendix~\\ref{app:decision-framework}). When $f_{\\text{death}} > 75\\%$ (as in ScienceWorld, where all four conditions validate), fixed deterministic routing is optimal; below $50\\%$, genuine routing opportunities warrant learned routers.\n\n\n",

_source line 1312 · 2026-05-21T11:52:01.549Z_

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   This is Director Loop #13 for simgate-modular-routing targeting EMNLP 2026 ARR (DDL 2026-05-25, 4 days remaining). The session focused on:
   - Processing and fixing all issues from internal review rounds (Round 20-24)
   - Maximizing paper quality within the strict 8-page main text constraint
   - Data consistency verification across all paper numbers vs registry
   - Reproducibility checklist compliance for EMNLP ARR
   - Writing-level improvements to boost Excitement from 3.0 (adding prescriptive guidance)
   - Managing infrastructure (vLLM kill, GPU allocation)
   - Waiting for user confirmation on: (a) anonymous repo, (b) internal review mode (continue/fresh), (c) ARR submission start

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Death Avoidance Factor (DAF)**: 88-90% across three model families; primary headline is penalty-independent label fraction 78.9% (75 of 95 non-tie labels)
   - **Episode-level counterfactual framework**: Decomposes routing signal before router construction
   - **Step tie rate**: 97.4% (5,935 of 6,094 steps) — critical metric showing per-step oracle degeneracy
   - **EMNLP 2026 ARR**: 8-page main text hard limit; Limitations/Ethics/References/Appendix unlimited
   - **Page budget management**: Extremely tight — every word change requires compile + visual page check
   - **Three model families**: Qwen2.5-7B (DAF 90.4%, CI [81.1%, 99.0%]), Llama-3.1-8B (DAF 88.2%, CI [80.2%, 95.1%]), Gemma-2-9B-it (DAF 89.7%, CI [79.5%, 98.4%])
   - **Bootstrap CI source**: `unified_episode_bootstrap.py` (B=10,000) is canonical; old per-model `analysis.json` values in registry are non-authoritative
   - **Excitement 3.0 structural ceiling**: Diagnostic papers inherently score lower on Excitement; writing improvements estimated +0.15-0.25 max
   - **Decision D131**: No new experiments in final 4 days — risk of bugs outweighs marginal Excitement gain

3. Files and Code Sections:

     \paragraph{Anatomy of catastrophic failure.}
     All 105 internal-strategy deaths trace to a single grounding failure: misuse of the answer-submission action (\texttt{focus on}) as a general interaction command.      \paragraph{Practical implications.}
     Death dominance diagnosis is actionable: a ${\sim}$50-episode pilot suffices to compute $f_{\text{death}}$ and classify the environment before committing to router development (Appendix~\ref{app:decision-framework}).         "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Why the router fails: task-type-level signal.}\nThe router diagnostic (\\S\\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}) reveals a structural mismatch: death avoidance is a task-type-level binary property (10/11 types carry death risk), but step-level features correlate weakly because death risk depends on multi-step trajectories. The 4B router improves at episode level ($59.0\\%$) without step-level gains ($56$--$59\\%$), confirming task-type classification; cross-task-type transfer yields chance accuracy (49.2\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:ablations}).\n\n\\paragraph{Non-triviality of death dominance.}\nWhile death dominance follows from the penalty structure (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}), three specific consequences were not predictable a priori: (i)~$10{\\times}$ scaling to 72B fails to eliminate it (DAF${}= 92.4\\%$); (ii)~Reflexion self-correction yields DAF${}>100\\%$, meaning death avoidance \\emph{more} than accounts for the full gap; (iii)~a 0.5B model shows \\emph{reduced} death rate ($13.3\\%$; $n{=}15$) because it is too weak to reach dangerous states, revealing an inverted-U capability--vulnerability relationship. All four intervention axes leave ${\\geq}12$~pp residual gaps to deterministic safety (Appendix~\\ref{sec:threshold-routing}); cross-model stability ($36$--$43\\%$ internal DR across three families) confirms death vulnerability is a task-environment property. More aggressive interventions remain untested, so we characterize a ceiling, not a universal bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Anatomy of catastrophic failure.}\nAll 105 internal-strategy deaths trace to a single grounding failure: misuse of the answer-submission action (\\texttt{focus on}) as a general interaction command. The internal strategy enters catastrophic states more frequently (105 vs.\\ 40 deaths), not through different within-episode behavior; grounding interventions reduce but do not eliminate death (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}--\\ref{app:grounding-ablation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Practical implications.}\nDeath dominance diagnosis is actionable: a ${\\sim}$50-episode pilot suffices to compute $f_{\\text{death}}$ and classify the environment before committing to router development (Appendix~\\ref{app:decision-framework}). When $f_{\\text{death}} > 75\\%$ (as in ScienceWorld, where all four conditions validate; thresholds are environment-specific, Appendix~\\ref{app:decision-framework}), fixed deterministic routing matches or exceeds learned alternatives; below $50\\%$, genuine opportunities warrant router development.\n\n\n",
        "numLines": 17,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 17
      }
    },

width, det_pref, width, label='Det-preferred',\n               color='#c0392b', edgecolor='white', linewidth=0.5)\nbars2 = ax.bar(x, int_pref, width, label='Int-preferred',\n               color='#27ae60', edgecolor='white', linewidth=0.5)\nbars3 = ax.bar(x + width, no_pref, width, label='No-preference',\n               color='#bdc3c7', edgecolor='white', linewidth=0.5, alpha=0.7)\n\nax.set_ylabel('Proportion (%)')\nax.set_xticks(x)\nax.set_xticklabels(envs)\nax.legend(loc='upper center', ncol=3, frameon=False, bbox_to_anchor=(0.5, 1.12))\nax.set_ylim(0, 95)\nax.spines['top'].set_visible(False)\nax.spines['right'].set_visible(False)\n\nfor bars in [bars1, bars2, bars3]:\n    for bar in bars:\n        h = bar.get_height()\n        ax.annotate(f'{h:.1f}%',\n                    xy=(bar.get_x() + bar.get_width()/2, h),\n                    xytext=(0, 3), textcoords='offset points',\n                    ha='center', va='bottom', fontsize=7)\n\nax.axvline(x=1.5, color='#95a5a6', linestyle='--', linewidth=0.8, alpha=0.6)\n\nax.annotate('Catastrophic failure\\n(routing signal)',\n            xy=(0.5, 82), xycoords=('data', 'data'),\n            fontsize=7.5, ha='center', va='bottom', color='#2c3e50', style='italic')\nax.annotate('No catastrophic failure\\n(no signal)',\n            xy=(2.0, 82), xycoords=('data', 'data'),\n            fontsize=7.5, ha='center', va='bottom', color='#2c3e50', style='italic')\n\nout_path = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), 'signal_reversal.pdf')\nplt.tight_layout()\nplt.savefig(out_path, bbox_inches='tight', dpi=300)\nprint(f\"Saved: {out_path} ({os.path.getsize(out_path)} bytes)\")\n",

    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\nLarge language models deployed as interactive agents must repeatedly decide \\emph{how} to compute: reason internally, or delegate to an external tool.\nIn ScienceWorld \\cite{scienceworld-2022}, a text-based science simulation, internal reasoning is error-prone---the model may hallucinate state changes (e.g., that water has boiled when the stove was never activated)---while deterministic external computation eliminates such errors via exact state transitions. The practical question: \\emph{when should an agent route computation externally versus internally?}\n\nExisting routing approaches frame this as a capability or efficiency decision: Toolformer \\cite{toolformer-2023} inserts API calls by perplexity reduction; ART \\cite{art-2023} decomposes tasks into tool-augmented programs; Chameleon \\cite{chameleon-2023} selects heterogeneous modules per subtask; adaptive-computation methods route to different reasoning depths \\cite{cogrouter-2026,ares-2026}, model tiers \\cite{mtrouter-2026}, or episode-level model selection under sparse rewards \\cite{budget-aware-routing-2026}; concurrent analyses further presuppose diverse routing signals~\\citep{when2tool-2026,tool-use-tax-2026,to-call-or-not-2026}. The shared assumption---that routing signal stems from diverse sources and a learned router can exploit it---remains untested: no prior work has decomposed what drives routing decisions in interactive environments.\n\nWe propose a \\emph{diagnostic framework} comprising (i)~episode-level counterfactual labeling under pure strategies (\\S\\ref{sec:collection}--\\ref{sec:labeling}), and (ii)~the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF), which decomposes the routing score gap into death-avoidance and non-death components (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}). Applied to ScienceWorld, \\textbf{78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels arise from death asymmetry} (75 of 95), a penalty-independent finding. Under ScienceWorld's penalty ($P{=}100$), DAF reaches $90.4\\%$ (95\\% CI: $[81.1\\%, 99.0\\%]$). The internal strategy's hallucination-induced failures trigger catastrophic death ($-100$ penalty); once death episodes are removed, the gap collapses to $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} = +2.84$ (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}).\n\nThis addresses a methodological gap: per-step oracle comparison degenerates under sparse rewards (97.4\\% tie rate; \\S\\ref{sec:collection}), and keyword-based heuristics fail structurally (\\S\\ref{sec:baselines}). Our approach runs complete episodes under pure-internal (ReAct \\cite{react-2023}) and pure-deterministic strategies, comparing episode outcomes.\n\nCross-context checks probe specificity: MATH Level~4--5 ($N{=}795$, code interpreter) shows no routing signal without catastrophic failure ($73.5\\%$ vs.\\ $73.0\\%$, $p = 0.81$); Llama-3.1-8B replication ($N{=}220$) confirms death dominance across model families (direction consistency 10/10; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}). Four intervention axes (retry, Reflexion~\\citep{reflexion-2023}, $10{\\times}$ scaling, action-space restriction) reduce death rates but none closes the gap to deterministic safety, with ${\\geq}$12~pp residual gaps (\\S\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}).\nAn exploratory router (Qwen3 LoRA, 0.6B--4B) yields flat step-level balanced accuracy ($56$--$59\\%$); at episode level, only 4B exceeds the always-deterministic baseline (\\S\\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}).\n\nOur contributions: (1)~an \\textbf{episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework} that overcomes per-step oracle degeneracy (97.4\\% tie rate), decomposes routing signal via penalty-independent (label fraction) and penalty-dependent (DAF) metrics, and predicts routing value from a ${\\sim}$50-episode pilot (validated 4/4 conditions; Appendix~\\ref{app:decision-framework}); (2)~applying this framework to ScienceWorld, we find that \\textbf{78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels} stem from death asymmetry---a single mechanism that persists across four intervention axes and three model scales, contradicting the routing literature's implicit assumption of diverse signal sources (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}); (3)~\\textbf{cross-model validation} (Llama-3.1-8B, DAF${=}88.2\\%$; Gemma-2-9B-it, DAF${=}89.7\\%$), four intervention axes, and cross-environment boundary probes (MATH, ALFWorld) confirm the framework's diagnostic utility and the robustness of the finding (\\S\\ref{sec:math-contrast}--\\ref{sec:cross-model}).\n\n\n",
        "numLines": 18,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 18
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/introduction.tex"

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_019FXxBoCceRYL4RCVmiUx2v`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Why the router fails: task-type-level signal.}
5	The router diagnostic (\S\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}) reveals a structural mismatch: death avoidance is a task-type-level binary property (10/11 types carry death risk), but step-level features correlate weakly because death risk depends on multi-step trajectories. The 4B router improves at episode level ($59.0\%$) without step-level gains ($56$--$59\%$), confirming task-type classification; cross-task-type transfer yields chance accuracy (49.2\%; Appendix~\ref{app:ablations}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Non-triviality of death dominance.}
8	While death dominance follows from the penalty structure (\S\ref{sec:daf}), three specific consequences were not predictable a priori: (i)~$10{\times}$ scaling to 72B fails to eliminate it (DAF${}= 92.4\%$); (ii)~Reflexion self-correction yields DAF${}>100\%$, meaning death avoidance \emph{more} than accounts for the full gap; (iii)~a 0.5B model shows \emph{reduced} death rate ($13.3\%$; $n{=}15$) because it is too weak to reach dangerous states, revealing an inverted-U capability--vulnerability relationship. All four intervention axes leave ${\geq}12$~pp residual gaps to deterministic safety (Appendix~\ref{sec:threshold-routing}); cross-model stability ($36$--$43\%$ internal DR across three families) confirms death vulnerability is a task-environment property. More aggressive interventions remain untested, so we characterize a ceiling, not a universal bound.
9	
10	\paragraph{Anatomy of catastrophic failure.}
11	All 105 internal-strategy deaths trace to a single grounding failure: misuse of the answer-submission action (\texttt{focus on}) as a general interaction command. The internal strategy enters catastrophic states more frequently (105 vs.\ 40 deaths), not through different within-episode behavior; grounding interventions reduce but do not eliminate death (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}--\ref{app:grounding-ablation}).
12	
13	\paragraph{Practical implications.}
14	Death dominance diagnosis is actionable: a ${\sim}$50-episode pilot suffices to compute $f_{\text{death}}$ and classify the environment before committing to router development (Appendix~\ref{app:decision-framework}). When $f_{\text{death}} > 75\%$ (as in ScienceWorld, where all four conditions validate; thresholds are environment-specific, Appendix~\ref{app:decision-framework}), fixed deterministic routing matches or exceeds learned alternatives; below $50\%$, genuine opportunities warrant router development.
15	
16	
17	
```

</details>

Still overflowing! Page 8 ends with "Cross-environment" at the very bottom, and page 9 starts with "probes confirm generality; a ~50-episode diagnostic pilot suffices for build-vs-buy decisions in new environments."

The trim I made to the Discussion was not enough — it saved maybe half a line but I needed a full line. I need to trim more aggressively.

Options:
1. Trim the Conclusion itself — make it shorter
2. Trim more from Discussion
3. Both

Let me look at the Conclusion text:
```
In ScienceWorld, a single mechanism---death avoidance---accounts for 78.9\% of non-tie routing labels, stable across three model families (DAF${=}88$--$90\%$); our episode-level counterfactual framework diagnoses this before any router is built, challenging the routing literature's implicit assumption of diverse signal sources. 

Cross-environment probes confirm generality; a ${\sim}$50-episode diagnostic pilot suffices for build-vs-buy decisions in new environments.
```

The overflow is just the end of the second sentence. I could:

Option A: Shorten "challenging the routing literature's implicit assumption of diverse signal sources" → "challenging the assumption that routing signal is diverse"

Option B: Make Conclusion one sentence by merging both ideas and cutting the second sentence entirely.

Option A seems like the minimal fix. Let me try that first. I'm trying to trim the conclusion by simplifying some key phrases—replacing "death avoidance alone" for the longer phrasing and cutting "the routing literature's implicit assumption of diverse signal sources" down to just "the assumption of diverse signal sources." That should save roughly a line in the two-column format without losing the core message about generality and the diagnostic pilot approach.
````

When $f_{\\text{death}} > 75\\%$ (as in ScienceWorld, where all four conditions validate), fixed deterministic routing matches or exceeds learned alternatives; below $50\\%$, genuine opportunities warrant router development.",
    "newString": "Death dominance diagnosis is actionable: a ${\\sim}$50-episode pilot classifies the environment before committing to router development (Appendix~\\ref{app:decision-framework}).     "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Why the router fails: task-type-level signal.}\nThe router diagnostic (\\S\\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}) reveals a structural mismatch: death avoidance is a task-type-level binary property (10/11 types carry death risk), but step-level features correlate weakly because death risk depends on multi-step trajectories. The 4B router improves at episode level ($59.0\\%$) without step-level gains ($56$--$59\\%$), confirming task-type classification; cross-task-type transfer yields chance accuracy (49.2\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:ablations}).\n\n\\paragraph{Non-triviality of death dominance.}\nWhile death dominance follows from the penalty structure (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}), three specific consequences were not predictable a priori: (i)~$10{\\times}$ scaling to 72B fails to eliminate it (DAF${}= 92.4\\%$); (ii)~Reflexion self-correction yields DAF${}>100\\%$, meaning death avoidance \\emph{more} than accounts for the full gap; (iii)~a 0.5B model shows \\emph{reduced} death rate ($13.3\\%$; $n{=}15$) because it is too weak to reach dangerous states, revealing an inverted-U capability--vulnerability relationship. All four intervention axes leave ${\\geq}12$~pp residual gaps to deterministic safety (Appendix~\\ref{sec:threshold-routing}); cross-model stability ($36$--$43\\%$ internal DR across three families) confirms death vulnerability is a task-environment property. More aggressive interventions remain untested, so we characterize a ceiling, not a universal bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Anatomy of catastrophic failure.}\nAll 105 internal-strategy deaths trace to a single grounding failure: misuse of the answer-submission action (\\texttt{focus on}) as a general interaction command. The internal strategy enters catastrophic states more frequently (105 vs.\\ 40 deaths), not through different within-episode behavior; grounding interventions reduce but do not eliminate death (Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}--\\ref{app:grounding-ablation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Practical implications.}\nDeath dominance diagnosis is actionable: a ${\\sim}$50-episode pilot suffices to compute $f_{\\text{death}}$ and classify the environment before committing to router development (Appendix~\\ref{app:decision-framework}). When $f_{\\text{death}} > 75\\%$ (as in ScienceWorld, where all four conditions validate), fixed deterministic routing matches or exceeds learned alternatives; below $50\\%$, genuine opportunities warrant router development.\n\n\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01BH9TYGoPJEMaoQQfffqpCU`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Why the router fails: task-type-level signal.}
5	The router diagnostic (\S\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}) reveals a structural mismatch: death avoidance is a task-type-level binary property (10/11 types carry death risk), but step-level features correlate weakly because death risk depends on multi-step trajectories. The 4B router improves at episode level ($59.0\%$) without step-level gains ($56$--$59\%$), confirming task-type classification; cross-task-type transfer yields chance accuracy (49.2\%; Appendix~\ref{app:ablations}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Non-triviality of death dominance.}
8	While death dominance follows from the penalty structure (\S\ref{sec:daf}), three specific consequences were not predictable a priori: (i)~$10{\times}$ scaling to 72B fails to eliminate it (DAF${}= 92.4\%$); (ii)~Reflexion self-correction yields DAF${}>100\%$, meaning death avoidance \emph{more} than accounts for the full gap; (iii)~a 0.5B model shows \emph{reduced} death rate ($13.3\%$; $n{=}15$) because it is too weak to reach dangerous states, revealing an inverted-U capability--vulnerability relationship. All four intervention axes leave ${\geq}12$~pp residual gaps to deterministic safety (Appendix~\ref{sec:threshold-routing}); cross-model stability ($36$--$43\%$ internal DR across three families) confirms death vulnerability is a task-environment property. More aggressive interventions remain untested, so we characterize a ceiling, not a universal bound.
9	
10	\paragraph{Anatomy of catastrophic failure.}
11	All 105 internal-strategy deaths trace to a single grounding failure: misuse of the answer-submission action (\texttt{focus on}) as a general interaction command. The internal strategy enters catastrophic states more frequently (105 vs.\ 40 deaths), not through different within-episode behavior; grounding interventions reduce but do not eliminate death (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}--\ref{app:grounding-ablation}).
12	
13	\paragraph{Practical implications.}
14	Death dominance diagnosis is actionable: a ${\sim}$50-episode pilot classifies the environment before committing to router development (Appendix~\ref{app:decision-framework}). When $f_{\text{death}} > 75\%$ (validated in ScienceWorld), deterministic routing matches or exceeds learned alternatives; below $50\%$, genuine opportunities warrant router development.
15	
16	
17	
```
